(2) A person who commits an offence under subsection (1) is liable—
(a) on summary conviction, to a fine not exceeding twenty-five thousand
dollars and on a second or subsequent offence to a fine not exceeding
fifty thousand dollars; and
(b) on conviction or indictment, to a fine not exceeding fifty thousand
dollars, and on a second or subsequent offence, to a fine not exceeding
one hundred thousand dollars.
(3) In determining the amount of any fine to be imposed on a person convicted
of an offence under subsection (1), the Court shall, in particular, have regard to any
financial benefit which has accrued or appears likely to accrue to the person in
consequence of the offence.
(4) For the purposes of subsection (1)(a)(v), a person shall be deemed to
display an advertisement if—
(a) the advertisement is displayed on the land of which he or she is the
owner or occupier; or
(b) the advertisement gives publicity to his or her goods, trade, business or
other concerns.
(5) A person shall not be guilty of an offence under subsection (1)(a)(iv) by
reason only that an advertisement is displayed on land of which he or she is the
owner or occupier, or that his or her goods, trade, business or other concerns are
given publicity by the advertisement, if he or she proves that it was displayed without
his or her knowledge or concern.
(6) Where an offence under this Act is committed by a body corporate and is
proved to have been committed with the consent or connivance of any director,
manager, secretary or other similar officer of the body corporate, or of any person
who was purporting to act in such capacity, he or she, as well as the body corporate,
shall be guilty of that offence and are liable to be proceeded against and punished
accordingly.
(7) Proceedings in respect of an offence alleged to have been committed under
this Act may be brought, with the approval of the Board, by the Chief Physical
Planner, provided that if it is considered that the gravity of the offence requires that it
be tried on indictment, proceedings shall only be brought by the Director of Public
Prosecutions.
Regulations.

### 86.

(1) The Minister may make regulations for carrying into effect the provisions
of this Act.
(2) Without prejudice to the generality of subsection (1), the Minister may
make regulations for—

(a) development which may be permitted by Order made by the Minister
under section 20(2), without the requirement of express grant of the
development permission;
(b) the designation of use classes by all Order made by the Minister under
paragraph (h) of the definition of “development” in section 2(1);
(c) the procedures to be followed and the form of any form, notice, order
or other document authorised or required by this Act to be served,
made or issued, in connection with—
(i) applications for a grant of development permission,
(ii) consultations on applications for development permission,
(iii) the modification or revocation of a grant of development
permission,
(iv) compliance notices,
(v) claims for compensations;
(d) the procedures for environmental impact assessment and the form of
environmental impact statements;
(e) the regulation of subdivision of land;
(f) the making up of undeveloped private streets;
(g) the procedures for the conduct of public inquires held under section
29;
(h) the fees payable under this Act;
(i) the qualifications required of persons signing forms, plans and
drawings on behalf of any applicant for development permission and
the qualifications required of persons preparing environmental impact
statements;
(j) the control of advertisements;
(k) the preservation of buildings, sites or plants;
(l) the form of the register to be maintained under section 82.
Application of Act to National Housing Corporation; WhiteGate Development
Corporation.

### 87.

Notwithstanding anything contained in the National Housing Corporation Act,
Cap. 23.18 and the WhiteGate Development Corporation Act, Cap. 20.50 the
Corporations referred to in this section shall be subject to the jurisdiction and powers
of the Development Control and Planning Board established under this Act.
(Amended by S.R.O. 2/2006)

Application of Act to Frigate Bay Development Area.

### 88.

(1) Notwithstanding anything contained in the Frigate Bay Development
Corporation Act, Cap. 20.15, the Frigate Bay Development Area shall be subject to
this Act and Regulations made under this Act and to the powers and authority of the
Development Control and Planning Board.
(2) Wherever there is inconsistency between this Act and any other Act the
provisions of this Act shall prevail.
(Amended by Act 3 of 2006)

———————

## FIRST SCHEDULE

(Section 6)
CONSTITUTION AND PROCEDURES OF THE SAINT CHRISTOPHER
DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING BOARD
Membership of Board.

1\.
(1) The Board shall consist of thirteen members appointed by the Minister of
whom—
(a) the following persons shall be ex-officio members—
(i) the Chief Physical Planner,
(ii) the Director of the Planning Unit,
(iii) the Chief Environmental Health Officer,
(iv) the Chief Technical Officer of the Department of Lands and
Housing,
(v) the Director of Public Works Department,
(vi) the Director of Environment,
(vii) the Chief Water Engineer,
(viii)the Chief Engineer/Manager of Electricity,
(ix) the Commissioner of Police, and
(x) the General Manager of St Kitts Sugar Manufacturing
Corporation.
(b) three members shall be persons not in the public service who shall be
appointed from among persons who have knowledge and experience
in
(i) commerce and industry,
(ii) economic and financial matters,
(iii) any other area of public interest that the Minister considers
relevant to physical planning.
(2) The Minister shall by instrument in writing appoint a chairperson and a
deputy chairperson of the Board from amongst the non-ex officio members and the
deputy chairperson shall in the absence, for any reason, of the chairperson, perform
the functions of the chairperson.
(3) A member appointed under sub-paragraph (1)(b) shall hold office for a
period of three years, but shall be eligible for reappointment upon the expiry of his or
her period of office.
(4) Every appointment made under sub-paragraph (1) and (2) shall be notified
in the Gazette.
Board.

(5) The Chief Physical Planner shall be the chief executive officer of the

(6) In the event of the temporary incapacity of a member, whether by reason
of illness or other sufficient cause, or the temporary absence from Saint Christopher

of any member, the Minister may appoint some other person to act as a temporary
member for so long as the incapacity or absence continues.
(7) Any member, save an ex-officio member, of the Board may at anytime, by
notice in writing to the Minister, resign his or her office.
(8) The Minister may remove from office any member of the Board for
inability, misbehaviour, or on the ground of any employment or interest which is
incompatible with the functions of a member of the Board or for any other good
cause.
Meetings of the Board.

2\.
(1) The Board shall meet at least once in every month and at such other times
as may be necessary for the transaction of business, at such places and times and on
such dates as the Board may determine.
(2) The chairperson shall convene a special meeting of the Board within seven
days of receipt of a requisition or that purpose addressed to him or her in writing and
signed by any five member of the Board, and on any other occasion when he or she is
directed in writing by the Minister so to do.
(3) The chairperson shall preside at meetings of the Board.
(4) The chairperson and seven other members shall form a quorum, provided
that where any member is disqualified by virtue of paragraph 4 from taking part in
any deliberation or decision at any meeting of the Board, that member shall be
disregarded for the purpose of constituting a quorum or decision.
How decisions to be taken.
3\.
The decision of the Board with regard to any question shall be determined by a
majority of votes of the members present and voting at a meeting of the Board, and in
the event of an equality of votes the chairperson shall, in addition to his or her own
original vote, have a casting vote.
Declaration of interest.

4\.
(1) There shall be an item of business on the agenda of each meeting which
shall be named “declaration of interest” and under this item—
(a) the chairperson shall draw the attention of members and officers in
attendance, to the law relating to conflict of interest; and
(b) members and officers in attendance shall declare their interest in any
item of business on the agenda.
(2) In respect of any item of business on which they have declared an interest,
the Chairperson, or where he or she has taken the chair, the vice-chairperson, or
where a member has taken the chair, that member, shall vacate the chair for that item
of business.
(3) A member or an officer in attendance who has declared an interest in an
item of business to be discussed at a meeting shall, when that item of business is
reached on the agenda, leave the meeting while that item of business is being
discussed and shall take no part directly or indirectly in any decision, deliberation,
discussion, consideration or other like activity on that matter.
(4) Where, owing to the number of members who have declared an interest in
an item of business at a meeting, the Board lacks a quorum to transact that item of

business, that item of business shall be deferred to the subsequent meeting, and the
fact shall be recorded in the minutes and reported to the Minister.
Validity of Boards actions.
5\.
The validity of anything done under this Act shall not be affected solely by
reason of—

(a) the existence of any vacancy in the membership, or any defect in the
constitution of the Board; or
(b) an omission or irregularity in respect of any meeting or proceedings of
the Board.
Defraying of expenses.
6\.
The expenses of the Board shall be defrayed out of sums provided for the
purpose in the annual estimates of revenue and expenditure for Saint Christopher and
shall be a charge on the Consolidated Fund.
Definition of chairperson.
7\.
In this Schedule “chairperson” includes a person appointed or elected, as the
case may be, to act temporarily in place of the chairperson.
_________

## SECOND SCHEDULE

(Section 11(5)(d))
MATTERS FOR WHICH PROVISION MAY BE MADE IN
DEVELOPMENT PLANS

## PART I

ROADS
1\. Reservation of land for roads and establishment of public rights of way
including public rights of way to and over beaches.
2\. Closing or diversion of existing roads and public and private rights of way.
3\. Construction of new roads and alteration of existing roads.
4\. The line, width, level, construction, access to and egress from and the
general dimensions and character of roads, whether new or existing.
5\. Providing for and generally regulating the construction or execution of
works incidental to the making or improvement of any road, including the erection of
bridges, culverts, gullies, fencing, barriers, shelters, the provision of artificial
lighting, and seats and the planting or protecting of grass, trees and shrubs on or
adjoining such road.

## PART II

BUILDING AND OTHER STRUCTURES
1\. Regulating and controlling, either generally or in particular areas, all or
any of the following matters—
(a) the size and height of buildings and fences;
(b) building lines, coverage and the space about buildings;
(c) the objects which may be affixed to buildings;
(d) the purposes for and the manner in which buildings may be used or
occupied, including in the case of dwelling houses, the letting thereof
in separate tenements;
(e) the prohibition of building or other operations on any land, or
regulating such operations.
2\. Regulating and controlling the design, colour and materials of buildings
and fences.
3\. Allocating any particular land, or all land in any particular area, for
buildings of a specified class or classes or prohibiting or restricting either
permanently or temporarily, the making of any building or any particular class or
classes of buildings on any specified land.
4\. Limiting the number of buildings or the number of buildings of a specified
class which may be constructed, erected or made, on, in or under any area.

## PART III

COMMUNITY PLANNING
1\. Providing for the control of land by zoning or designating specific uses.
2\. Regulating the layout of housing areas, including, density, spacing,
grouping and orientation of houses in relation to roads, open spaces and other
buildings.
3\. Determining the provision and siting of community facilities including
shops, schools, churches, meeting halls, play centres and recreation grounds in
relation to the number and siting of houses.

## PART IV

AMENITIES
1\. Allocation of lands as open spaces, whether public or private.
2\. Allocation of land for burial grounds and crematoria.
3\. Allocation of lands—
(a) for communal parks;
(b) for game and bird sanctuaries;
(c) for the protection of marine life;
(d) for national parks and environmental protection areas.

4\. Preservation of buildings, caves, sites and objects of artistic, architectural,
cultural, historic or archaeological interest.
5\. Preservation or protection of forests, woods, trees, shrubs, plants and
flowers.
6\. Protection of coastal zone, designation of marine parks, special resource
and special use areas.
7\. Prohibiting, restricting or controlling, either generally or in particular
places, the exhibition, whether on the ground, or any building or any temporary
erection, whether on land or in water, or in the air, of all or any particular forms of
advertisement or other public notices.
8\. Preventing, remedying or removing injury to amenities arising from the
ruinous or neglected condition of any building or fence, or by objectionable or
neglected condition of any land attached to a building or fence, or abutting on a road
or situated in a residential area.
9\. Prohibiting, regulating and controlling the deposit or disposal of waste
materials and refuse, the disposal of sewage and the pollution of rivers, lakes, ponds,
gullies, beaches and seashore.

## PART V

PUBLIC SERVICES
1\. Facilitating the establishment, extension or improvement of works by
statutory or other undertakers in relation to power, lighting, water supply, sewerage,
drainage, sewage disposal, refuse disposal or other public services.

## PART VI

TRANSPORT AND COMMUNICATIONS
1\. Facilitating the establishment, extension or improvement of systems of
transport whether by land, water or air.
2\. Allocating sites for use in relation to transport, and the reservation of land
for that purpose.
3\. Providing for the establishment, extension or improvement of telegraphic,
telephonic, wireless or radar communication, the allocating of sites for use in relation
to such communication, and the reservation of land for that purpose.

## PART VII

MISCELLANEOUS
1\. Providing for and regulating the making of agreements for the purpose of a
development plan by the Minister with another body or owners and other persons,
and by another body with such persons and by such persons with one another.
2\. Sub-division of land and in particular, but without restricting the generality
of the foregoing—

(a) regulating the type of development to be carried out and the size and
form of plots;
(b) requiring the allocation of land for any of the public services referred
to in Part V or for any other purposes referred to in this Schedule for
which land may be allocated;
(c) prescribing the character and type of public services or other works
which shall be undertaken and completed by any applicant for
permission to sub-divide as a condition of the grant of such
permission;
(d) co-ordinating the sub-division of contiguous properties in order to give
effect to any scheme of development appertaining to such properties.
3\. Making any provision necessary for—
(a) adjusting and altering the boundaries and areas of any towns;
(b) enabling the establishment of satellite towns and new towns;
(c) effecting such exchanges of land or cancellation of existing subdivision plans as may be necessary or convenient for the purposes
referred to in subparagraphs (a) and (b).
__________

## THIRD SCHEDULE

(Section 26(1))
MATTERS FOR WHICH ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT ASSESSMENT
SHALL BE REQUIRED
1\. Hotels of more than twelve rooms;
2\. Sub-divisions of more than six plots;
3\. Residential development of more than six units;
4\. Any industrial plant which in the opinion of the Board is likely to cause
significant adverse environmental impact;
5\. Quarrying and other mining activities;
6\. Marinas;
7\. Land reclamation, dredging and filling of ponds;
8\. Airports, ports and harbours;
9\. Dams and reservoirs;
10.Hydro-electric projects and power plants;
11.Desalination plants;
12.Water purification plants;
13.Sanitary land fill operations, solid waste disposal sites, toxic waste disposal
sites and other similar sites;
14.Gas pipeline installations;
15.Any development projects generating or potentially generating emissions,
aqueous effluent, solid waste, noise/vibration or radioactive discharges;
16.Any development involving the storage and use of hazardous materials;
17.Coastal zone developments;

18.Development in wetlands, marine parks, national parks, conservation areas,
environmental protection areas or other sensitive environmental areas.
__________

## FOURTH SCHEDULE

(Section 20(2))
DEVELOPMENT CONTROL AND PLANNING (PERMITTED
DEVELOPMENT) ORDER, 2008
Citation.
1\.
This Order may be cited as the Development Control and Planning (Permitted
Development) Order.
Interpretation.
2\.
In this Order—

“Agreement” means the St Kitts Development Agreement which was finalized
between the Government of Saint Christopher and Nevis and the KHT Land
Holding Limited on 15 June 2007;
“Development Standards” means the St Kitts Peninsula Resort District Development
Control and Planning Standards as set out in the Agreement;
“Resort District” means St Kitts Peninsula Resort District as designated in the Saint
Christopher and Nevis (Designation of St Kitts Peninsula Resort District)
Order, No. 29/ 2007.
Permitted Development.
3\.
Permission is hereby granted for development to be carried out within the
Resort District in accordance with the Development Standards without the
requirement for the making of an application for the grant of express development
permission in respect of any development within the St. Kitts Peninsula Resort
District.

## FIFTH SCHEDULE

(Sections 52 and 86)
DEVELOPMENT CONTROL AND PLANNING (CONTROL OF
ADVERTISEMENTS) REGULATIONS

## PART I

PRELIMINARY
Citation.
1\.
These Regulations may be cited as the Development Control and Planning
(Control of Advertisements) Regulations.
Interpretation.
2\.
In these Regulations—

“Act” means the Development Control and Planning Act, Cap. 20.07;
“advertisement” means an advertisement as defined in section 2 of the Act;
“billboard’ means any sign, freestanding, fixed or movable, painted on or fixed to a
building, fence, wall, rock-face or other structure, that is used to advertise
products, goods or services not directly related to the primary use or activity
taking place on the site where the sign is located;
“Building Code” means the St. Kitts and Nevis Building Code set out in the Schedule
1 of the Building Regulations;
“directional sign” means any sign which is designed and erected solely for the
purpose of traffic or pedestrian direction, and which is placed on the property
to which the public is directed;
“electronic advertisement” means an advertisement on which the display can be
changed by electrical or electronic means and the display may be illuminated
by artificial means from a light source located within, above, behind, below or
near the advertisement;
“freestanding sign” means a sign which is supported from the ground and not
attached to or forming a part of a building;
“illuminated advertisement” means an advertisement that is designed or adapted to be
lit by artificial lighting directly or by reflection from a source located within,
above, behind, below or near the advertisement;
“off-premises sign” means a sign that advertises a product, an institution, an
organization, a business, a service, an events, a locations or a facility or directs
persons to premises different from where the sign is installed but does not
include governmental, traffic, directional or regulatory signs;
“on-premises sign” means a sign which carries only advertisements strictly related to
a lawful use of the premises on which it is located, including advertisement or
advertisement devices indicating the business transacted;
“portable advertisement” means any advertisement designed to move or be moved by
trailer or vehicle to advertise at different locations and may include
anyadvertisement which is not firmly fastened to a building or the ground;

“principal road” means a road designated as such pursuant to the Vehicles and Road
Traffic Act, Cap. 15.06;
“Standard Conditions” means the conditions specified in Schedule 2;
“traffic sign” means a traffic sign designated as such pursuant to the Roads Act, Cap.
15.05;
“zoning district” means the division of a city, town or village into districts or areas
such as commercial, residential, historical, industrial and institutional districts
to preserve a desired characteristic for each district.
Application.
3\.
These Regulations apply to the display of any advertisement on the Island of
Saint Christopher.
Power to be Exercised in the Interest of Amenity and Public Safety.

4\.
(1) The Board shall exercise its powers under these Regulations only in the
interests of amenity and public safety, taking into account any material factors and in
particular—
(a) in the case of amenity, the general characteristics of the locality,
including the presence of any features of architectural, cultural,
historic, archaeological, scientific or similar interest and the natural
beauty or scenic value of the locality;
(b) in the case of public safety—
(i) the safety of any person who may use any road, dock, harbour, or
airfield;
(ii) whether any display of advertisements is likely to obscure, or
hinder any road or traffic sign, or any aid to navigation by water
or air.
(2) In determining an application for development permission for the display
of an advertisement, or considering whether to make an order revoking or modifying
such development permission, the Board may have regard to any material change in
circumstances likely to occur within the period for which the development permission
is required or granted.
(3) Unless it appears to the Board to be required in the interests of amenity or
public safety, development permission for the display of an advertisement shall not
contain any limitation relating to the subject matter, content or design of what is to be
displayed.
(4) Development permission for the display of advertisements shall take effect
as permission for the use of the site for the purposes of the display, whether by the
erection of structures or otherwise, and for the benefit of any person interested in the
site.

## PART II

CONTROL OF ADVERTISING
Requirement for Development Permission.
5\.
Subject to regulation 6, no person shall display an advertisement except
pursuant to development permission granted by the Board pursuant to the Act and
these Regulations.
Exempt Advertisements.

6\.
(1) An advertisement which falls in any of the classes set out in Schedule 1 is
exempt from the requirement for application for development permission as set out in
regulation 5 and may be displayed without the making of an application for
development permission.
(2) An advertisement which is exempt from the requirements for development
permission pursuant to sub-regulation (1) shall comply with the requirements of
Schedules 2, 3 and 4.
(3) An advertisement relating specifically to a pending Parliamentary election
or a referendum under the National Assembly Elections Act, Cap. 2.01 is exempt
from the requirements for application for development permission except that such
advertisement shall be removed within 14 days after the close of the poll in the
election or referendum to which it relates.

## PART III

DEVELOPMENT PERMISSION TO DISPLAY ADVERTISEMENTS
Application for Development Permission to Display Advertisements.

7\.
(1) An application for development permission to display an advertisement
shall be made to the Board through the Chief Physical Planner in such form as may
be specified by the Board and shall include the following information—
(a) the height of the advertisement from the ground level and details of
any projection;
(b) the dimensions of the advertisement;
(c) where applicable, the type of illumination to be used;
(d) the colour and content of the advertisement;
(e) the exact position of the advertisement; and
(f) any other information that the Board may request from the applicant.
(2) The application shall be accompanied by the relevant fee as set out in
Schedule 5 and shall be accompanied by a certificate signed by the applicant stating
that he has notified the owner of the land to which the application relate, or the
owner’s duly authorised representative, of the application and that the owner or his
duly authorised representative does not object to the application.
Receipt of Application.
8\.
On receipt of an application for development permission to display an
advertisement, the Board shall—

(a) send an acknowledgement in writing to the applicant;
(b) may request the applicant, in writing, to provide it with such evidence
as may be reasonably called for to verify any particulars or
information given to it.
Duty to Consult.

9\.
(1) Before granting development permission to display an advertisement, the
Board may consult any person, department or body for the purpose of obtaining
reasonable information or such approval as the Board may determine necessary
before action is taken on the application.
(2) The Board shall give at least 14 days notice to those it intends to consult
that the relevant application is to be considered and shall take into account in dealing
with the application, any representations made in response to that consultation.
Power to deal with Applications.
10\. Where an application for development permission to display an advertisement
is made to the Board pursuant to regulation 7, it may—

(a) subject to regulation 13, grant development permission in whole or in
part subject to the standard conditions and subject to such additional
conditions as it may think fit; or
(b) refuse to grant the development permission.
Grant of Development Permission to Display an Advertisement.

11\. (1) Development permission may be granted, subject to such conditions as
may be specified, for—
(a) the display of a particular advertisement with or without illumination
as the applicant specifies;
(b) the use of a particular site for the display of advertisements in a
specified manner whether by reference to the number, siting, size or
illumination of the advertisements or the structures intended for such
display or the design or appearance of any such structure; or
(c) the retention of any display of advertisements or the continuation of
the use of a site begun before the date of the application.
(2) The conditions imposed on development permission pursuant to subregulation (1) may include conditions—
(a) regulating the display of advertisements to which the development
permission relates;
(b) regulating the use for the display of advertisements of the site to which
the application relates or any adjacent land under the control of the
applicant, or requiring the carrying out of works on any such land;
(c) requiring the removal of any advertisement or the discontinuance of
the use of any land authorised by the development permission at the
end of a specified period, and the carrying out of any works required
for the reinstatement of the land.
(3) When development permission has been granted by the Board, no person
may change, modify or alter the structural characteristics of the advertisement, or

otherwise deviate from the terms or conditions of the development permission
without prior approval of the Board.
(4) A written record of an approval granted pursuant to sub-regulation (3)
shall be entered upon the original application for development permission and shall
be maintained in the Register of the Board.
(5) Development permission for the display of an advertisement shall be for a
period of one year, unless—
(a) the development permission provides otherwise; or
(b) the removal or discontinuance of the advertisement is required under
any provision of the Act.
Notification of Decision.

12\. (1) The grant or refusal of development permission by the Board shall be
notified in writing to the applicant within ninety days from the date of receipt of the
application.
(2) Where no decision has been made within ninety days of receipt of the
application, the Chief Physical Planner shall notify the applicant in writing of the
progress made on the application and the extended date by which the decision is
likely to be made.
(3) The Board shall in writing state its reasons for—
(a) any refusal for development permission in whole or in part; or
(b) any decision to impose any condition on the development permission;
for the display of an advertisement.
(4) Where no decision is made within ninety days of receipt of the application
and no notification of an extended date has been issued to the applicant, that
application shall be deemed to have been refused.
Application of Renewal for Development Permission to Display an Advertisement.
13\. Development permission for the display of an advertisement may be renewed
by the Board upon an application made not more than six months prior to the
expiration of the development permission and upon the payment of the relevant fee as
specified in Schedule 5.
Appeal to Appeals Tribunal.
14\. Any person who is dissatisfied with the decision of the Board in any
application for development permission for the display of an advertisement may
appeal to the Appeals Tribunal pursuant to Part IX of the Act.
Revocation or Modification of Express Permit.
15\. The provisions of section 37 of the Act shall apply to any revocation or
modification of development permission for the display of an advertisement.
Compensation for Revocation or Modification.
16\. Where a notice pursuant to regulation 15 takes effect, the provisions of Part
VIII of the Act shall apply with regards to any claims for compensation.

## PART IV

STANDARDS FOR ADVERTISEMENTS
Standard Conditions.

17\. (1) An advertisement for which development permission has been granted
shall comply with the requirements of this Part and with the Standard Conditions set
out in Schedule 2.
(2) Pursuant to section 53(2) of the Act every billboard, placard, poster, wall
painting or other advertisement shall display legibly and prominently identify at all
times—
(a) the name of the person who displayed it or caused it to be displayed;
(b) the date and permit number of the grant of development permission to
use the building or other land for the purposes of the display of the
advertisement.
Maintenance, Construction and Appearance of Advertisements.
18\. An advertisement and all of its supports, structures, braces, guys, and anchors
and the sites upon which it is located, shall—

(a) be kept in good repair with the braces, bolts, clips, supporting frame,
and fastenings free from deterioration, insect infestation, rot, rust, or
loosening;
(b) be maintained in a safe, neat, clear, and attractive condition;
(c) be kept free from excessive rust, corrosion, peeling paint or other
surface deterioration; and
(d) comply with the wind-load specifications of the Building Code.
Electronic Advertisement.

19\.

(1) An electronic advertisement shall—
(a) be erected to comply with the Building Code; and
(b) be kept rain proof and have service holes fitted with waterproof covers
provided to each compartment of such advertisement.

(2) An electronic billboard erected at a principal road intersection shall be
setback at least two hundred feet from the intersection, as measured from the curb
and three hundred feet from any traffic lights situated at that intersection.
Illuminated Advertisement.

20\. (1) No advertisement shall be illuminated by or contain any flashing,
intermittent, rotating, or moving light, except for time, date and temperature
advertisement.
(2) Any illuminated advertisement shall use only a light of constant intensity
and shall not be placed or directed in such a manner as to project the beams or
illumination upon a public street, highway, sidewalk, or adjacent premises so as to
cause glare or reflection that may constitute a traffic hazard or nuisance.
(3) The Board shall not grant development permission for an illuminated
advertisement to be displayed in a residential area and an illuminated advertisement

may only be placed so as not to be a nuisance to any residents of any adjacent
residentially zoned property within fifty feet of the advertisement.
(4) The Board may—
(a) order a change in the illumination of any advertisement that becomes a
hazard or nuisance to the public; or
(b) require an advertisement to be screened, shielded, relocated or the
lighting adjusted or other measures introduced to mitigate any
interference with any adjacent residential property.
(5) For the purposes of sub-regulations (3) and (4), a “nuisance” or
“interference” shall be defined as a flashing light of such intensity as may interfere
with the peaceful occupancy of any resident of their home.
Free Standing Advertisement.
21\. The location and structural design of a freestanding advertisement shall not
interfere with the safe and efficient use of any off-street parking or any loading area,
including any aisle way and access driveway.

## PART V

SPECIAL CONTROL AREAS AND PROHIBITED ADVERTISEMENTS
Special Control Areas.

22\. (1) Pursuant to section 52 (5) (b) of the Act no billboards or other freestanding
signs may be displayed in any of the Special Control areas set out in Schedule 3
except if it is—
(a) for the purpose of announcement or direction in relation to buildings
or other land in the locality;
(b) reasonably required in the interest of public safety;
(c) required pursuant to any other enactment.
(2) The Minister may for any occasion that he may deem necessary and after
consultation with the Board, permit the display of advertisements in any of the areas
set out in Schedule 3.
Prohibited Advertisement.
23\. The display of any advertisement specified in Schedule 4 is prohibited.

## PART VI

MISCELLANEOUS
Register of Applications for Development Permission.

24\. (1) The Board shall maintain a Register of applications for development
permission for the display of advertisements which shall contain—
(a) the name and address of each applicant;

(b) a description of the site in respect of which the application is made;
(c) the particulars of the advertisement;
(d) the date of the decision of the Board in respect of the application;
(e) the date of any subsequent approval given in relation to the
application.
(2) The Register shall be kept in the form of a book and may also be kept in an
electronic form and shall include an index which may be in the form of a map.
(3) Any person who so requests shall be provided by the Chief Physical
Planner with a copy of any entry in the register upon payment of the fee specified in
Schedule 5.
Register of Grants for Development Permission.

25\. (1) The Board shall maintain a Register of development permissions granted
for the display of advertisements which shall contain the particulars of—
(a) the location of all advertisements;
(b) the name of the person who was granted the development permission
for the display of the advertisement;
(c) the date and permit number of the development permission for the
display of the advertisement;
(d) the date of expiry of the approval of the development permission; and
(e) any other relevant information.
(2) The Register shall be kept in the form of a book and may also be kept in an
electronic form and shall include a map which shall show the location of all the
advertisements for which development permission has been granted.
(3) Any person who so requests shall be provided, by the Chief Physical
Planner, with a copy of any entry in the register upon payment of the fee specified in
Schedule 5.
Powers of Entry.

26\. (1) The provisions of section 79 of the Act shall apply with regards to the
powers of entry of the Minister, any member of the Board, the Chief Physical Planner
or any person authorised by him or her.
Compliance.

27\. (1) Pursuant to section 52(1) (c) of the Act, the provisions of Part V of the Act
shall apply to any situation where it appears to the Board that—
(a) an advertisement displayed pursuant to a development permission
(i) is not in accordance with the information supplied in the
application for the development permission; or
(ii) is in violation of these Regulations; or
(b) there has been any misrepresentation in connection with the
application for the development permission.

(2) The Board may, in accordance with section 53 of the Act, remove or
obliterate any billboard, placard, poster, wall painting, or other advertisement which
in its opinion is displayed in contravention of these Regulations.
Removal of Advertisement.
28\. Except as provided in these Regulations, any person who has been granted
development permission for the display of an advertisement shall remove such
advertisement—

(a) upon the expiry of the grant of the development permission for the
display of the advertisement;
(b) when the function or the business it advertises has discontinued on the
premises on which the advertisement is located; or
(c) when the advertisement is not in compliance with the requirements of
these Regulations.
Offences.
29\. The provisions of section 85 of the Act shall apply with respect to any person
who displays an advertisement in contravention of these Regulations.
Transitional Provision.

30\. (1) This regulation applies to any advertisement which was displayed on the
date of the coming into operation of these Regulations in accordance with any
development permission granted for the display of such advertisement by the Board.
(2) Any advertisement to which sub-regulation (1) applies may continue to be
displayed for a period of one year after the date of the coming into operation of these
Regulations, except that an application shall be made to the Board for development
permission for the display of the advertisement before the expiry of the year.
__________

## SCHEDULE 1

TO THE REGULATIONS
(Regulation 6)
CLASSES OF ADVERTISEMENTS WHICH ARE EXEMPTED FROM THE REQUIREMENT
FOR DEVELOPMENT PERMISSION
Class 1 – Statutory advertisements
1\.
Any advertisements required to be displayed by Standing Order of the
National Assembly or by any enactment or any condition imposed by any enactment
or the exercise of any function including without prejudice to the generality of the
foregoing, advertisements the display of which is required as a condition of the valid
exercise of any power or the proper performance of any function given or imposed by
an enactment.
Class 2 – Advertisements of governmental departments and statutory bodies
2\.
An advertisement for a utility warning, utility identification for public use,
buried cable signs, no trespassing, no hunting, or other safety signs.
Class 3 – Traffic signs
3\.
Advertisements which are traffic signs employed wholly for the control,
guidance or safety of traffic and displayed in accordance with the Vehicles and Road
Traffic Act or any regulations made pursuant to that Act or any enactment relating
thereto.
Class 4 – An advertisement displayed on or in a vehicle
4\.
An advertisement displayed on or in a vehicle normally used as a moving
vehicle.
Class 5 - Flag Advertisements
5\.
An advertisement in the form of flag attached to a single flagstaff on the roof
of a building or within its cartilage which bears the name of the device of the person
occupying the building.
Class 6 – Any advertisements displayed inside a building or enclosed land
6\.
An advertisement which is displayed inside a building and is not visible
outside the building or the land.
Class 7 – An advertisement on business premises
7\.
An advertisement displayed wholly on business premises or on the forecourt of
a business premises wholly with reference to the business carried on, the goods sold,
or services provided or the name or qualifications of the person carrying on the
business or supplying the goods or services on those premises.
Class 8 – A window display or a merchandise advertisement
8\.
A window display or a merchandise advertisement not otherwise prohibited.
Class 9 – A non-commercial advertisement in any zoning districts
9\.
An advertisement—

(a) announcing any local event of a religious, educational, cultural,
political, social, or recreational character; or
(b) relating to any temporary matter in connection with an event or local
activity of such a character,
not being an event or activity promoted or carried on for commercial purposes.
Class 10 – Advertisement incorporated in the fabric of a building
10\. On condition that an advertisement fixed to or painted on a building is not to
be regarded as incorporated in its fabric and a hoarding or similar structure is to be
regarded as a building used principally for the display of advertisement.
Class 11 – Advertisement displayed on an article or on a container
11\. An advertisement displayed on an article for sale or on the container in or from
which an article is sold.
Class 12 – Miscellaneous temporary advertisements

12\. (1) An advertisement relating to the sale, letting for residential, agricultural,
industrial or commercial use or for development for such use of the land or premises
on which it is displayed.
(2) An advertisement announcing the sale of goods or a garage sale where the
advertisement is displayed on the land where the goods are situated or where the sale
is held not being land which is normally used, whether at regular intervals or
otherwise for the holding of such sales on condition that—
(a) such advertisement does not exceed two square feet per advertisement
face; and
(b) such sale do not become a nuisance to the public.
(3) An advertisement announcing the giveaway of pets where the
advertisement is displayed on the land where the pets are situated or where the
giveaway is held not being land which is normally used, whether at regular intervals
or otherwise for the holding of such sales on condition that—
(a) such advertisement does not exceed two square feet per advertisement
face; and
(b) such advertisement does not become a nuisance to the public.
(4) An advertisement announcing the sale of goods or a garage sale
advertisement provided that the advertisement shall not exceed two square feet per
advertisement face and shall be removed within one day of such sale.
__________

1.1 In computing the area of an advertisement the standard mathematical
formulas for common regular geometric shapes shall be used.
1.2 In the case of an irregularly shaped advertisement or an advertisement
with letters or symbols directly affixed to or painted on the wall of a
building, the area of the advertisement shall be the entire area within

the perimeter or not more than eight straight lines enclosing the
extreme limits of writing, representation, emblem, or any figure of
similar character, together with any material or color forming an
integral part of a background of the display or as used to differentiate
such advertisement from the backdrop or structure against which it is
placed.
1.3 Where an advertisement has two or more display faces, the area of all
faces shall be included in determining the area of the advertisement.
2\.
The maximum height of a billboard shall not exceed 22 ft. as measured from
the surface of the road to the top of the board.
3\.
The vertical clearance beneath a billboard shall be a minimum of 6 ft. from the
underside of the sign to the surface of the ground.
4\.
A billboard shall not exceed a maximum area of 128 sq. ft.
5\.
The density of billboards or spacing between advertisements shall seek to
avoid clutter, minimize the extent of visual intrusion and maintain visibility of traffic
signs and signals.
6\.
Billboards along principal roads shall have a minimum distance of 50ft
between any two billboards.
7\.
All advertisements shall be sufficiently setback so as not to obstruct the line of
sight of motorists from or of any intersection, corner, bend or driveway or to impede
the view of any traffic signs or signals and the setbacks shall be measured from the
outer edge of the display board or the pole that supports it as follows:
7.1. The advertisements shall be setback a minimum of 20 ft. from a front
parcel boundary; 15 ft. from a rear parcel boundary, within any parcel
located along a principal road.
7.2. The advertisements shall be setback a minimum of 200 ft. from an
intersection as measured from the edge of the curb.
7.3 The advertisements shall be setback a minimum of 200 ft. from any
dangerous curve or bend in the road.
7.4 The advertisements shall not be placed within 200 ft. of any traffic
lights, traffic signs or other road safety or public information and
directional signs.
7.5 The advertisements shall be setback a minimum of 100 ft. from the
edge of the road reserve of a principal road.
7.6 The advertisements shall be setback a minimum of 100 ft. from any
church, school, cemetery, public park, playground or recreational area.
7.7 The advertisements shall be setback a minimum of 200 ft. from any
residential structure.
8\.
All advertisements shall be setback a minimum of 50 ft. from any other
structure, including utility infrastructure or any greater distance that may be specified
by the utility provider.
9\.
An advertisement advertising a business which is not conducted on the
premises, or a commodity or service which is not the primary product of sale or
services on the premises, shall not face or be oriented toward any adjoining or
abutting residential district.
10\. Non-commercial advertisement may be erected and maintained in any zoning
district subject to the following conditions—

(a) the maximum number of the non-commercial advertisements shall not
exceed one advertisement for each 100 feet of front lot line;
(b) the maximum advertisement area of non-commercial advertisement
shall be no more than 32 square feet total advertisement area;
(c) the maximum height above grade shall not exceed eight feet;
(d) the advertisement shall meet the setback requirements for the zoning
district in which they are located;
(e) the location of all such advertisements over three square feet per
face,except poster-type signs, shall be registered with the Board as to
their location, and date of erection.

## SCHEDULE 3

TO THE REGULATIONS
(Regulation 22)
SPECIAL CONTROL AREAS
The display of billboards and freestanding signs in the following locations is
prohibited below:
1\.
On the coastal and landward side of the Basseterre Bay Road and any other
coastal road.
2\.
Along the Dr. Kennedy Simmonds Highway.
3\.
The West Side Bus and Ferry terminal.
4\.
Along the John F Kennedy Highway also known as the Frigate Bay Road.
5\.
Along the Kim Collins Highway.
6\.
Any area of natural beauty, scenic or historical value as described in the Saint
Christopher National Physical Development Plan prepared by the Board in
accordance with the Act, or where such billboard or free standing sign may block
the view of any such areas.
7\.
Within any area designated as an environmental protection area by the Board
pursuant to the Act.
8\.
Within any national park designated pursuant to the National Conservation and
Environment Protection Act, Cap. 11.03.
9\.
Along watercourses and in or adjacent to wetlands and forested areas.
10\. In the Central Business District and the downtown area of Basseterre.
11\. In the Green Lands Park and the grass verge along Cayon Street in the Green
Lands Area.
12\. Within the compounds of any school or other educational facility.
13\. Within the confines of any golf course.
14\. On any telecommunication tower or utility pole.
15\. Along any traffic round-about.
16\. Within a ¼ mile radius of the Robert Llewellyn Bradshaw Airport and the
Deep-water Port.
__________

## SCHEDULE 4

TO THE REGULATIONS
(Regulation 23)
PROHIBITED ADVERTISEMENT
The erection, display or maintenance of advertisements having any of the following
characteristics is prohibited:
1\.
Any advertisement erected, or attached in any form, shape or manner to a fire
escape or any door or window giving access to any fire escape.
2\.
Any advertisement advertising activities that is illegal under any law or
regulations in effect at the location of such signs or at the locations of such activities.
3\.
Any advertisement which does not bear the names of the person granted the
development permission for the display of the advertisement.
4\.
Any advertisement artificially illuminated which is of such intensity or placed
in such a manner as to interfere with, or impair the vision of the driver of a motor
vehicle, or otherwise interfere with any driver’s operation of a motor vehicle.
5\.
An advertisement which attempts or appears to attempt to direct the movement
of traffic by interfering with, imitating or resembling any traffic sign, signal or
device.
6\.
An advertisement which prevent the driver of a vehicle from having a clear
and unobstructed view of traffic signs and approaching or merging traffic or any
other potential vehicular and pedestrian hazard.
7\.
Except for a banner, an advertisement which exceed a height of twenty feet.
8\.
An advertisement located or projecting within a right-of-way unless a street
use permit has been obtained from the relevant authority.
9\.
An advertisement with visible moving, revolving, rotating parts, or visible
mechanical movement of any description or other apparent visible movement
achieved by electrical, electronic, or mechanical means, except for time, temperature,
and date advertisement.
10\. An advertisement which incorporates projected images or emits any sound
which is intended to attract attention, or involve the use of live animals.
11\. Any advertisement or advertisement structure which—
(a) is structurally unsafe;
(b) constitutes a hazard to safety or health by reason of inadequate
maintenance or dilapidation
(c) is not kept in good repair;
(d) is capable of causing electrical shocks to persons likely to come into
contact with it;
(e) in any other way obstructs the view of, may be confused with, or
purports to be a traffic sign, signal, or device or any other official sign;
(f) uses any words, phrases, symbols, or characters implying the existence
of danger or the need for stopping or maneuvering of a motor vehicle;
(g) creates, in any other way, an unsafe distraction for vehicle operators or
pedestrians;

(h) obstructs the view of vehicle operators or pedestrians entering a public
roadway from any parking area, service drive, public driveway, alley,
or other thoroughfare; or
(i) is located on a tree, electricity pole or utility pole except where
required by law.
12\. Any advertisement which contains words, statements or pictures that may be
considered to be obscene, pornographic, libellous, threatening, sacrilegious or in any
other way offensive or discriminatory.

## SIXTH SCHEDULE

(Section 71(3))
CONSTITUTION AND PROCEDURES OF THE APPEALS TRIBUNAL
Membership of Appeals Tribunal.

1\.
(1) The Appeals Tribunal shall consist of five members appointed by the
Governor-General of whom the chairperson shall be a legal practitioner of not less
than ten years standing, and the other four members shall be appointed from among
persons trained and experienced in—
(a) physical planning;
(b) architecture;
(c) engineering;
(d) environmental, coastal and marine matters.

(2) A member of the Appeal Tribunal shall hold office for a period not
exceeding two years but such a member shall be eligible for reappointment.
(3) The names of all members of the Appeals Tribunal as first constituted and
every change in the membership thereof shall be published in the Gazette.
(4) Any member of the Appeals Tribunal may resign his or her office at
anytime by notice in writing to the Governor-General.
(5) The Governor-General may remove from office any member of the
Appeals Tribunal for inability, misbehaviour, or on the ground of any employment or
interest which is incompatible with the functions of a member of the Appeals
Tribunal.
Staff of Appeals Tribunal.

2\.
(1) The Governor-General shall appoint a secretary to the Appeals Tribunal
and such other officers as may be necessary to provide assistance to the Appeals
Tribunal.
(2) The acts of the Appeals Tribunal shall be authenticated by the signature of
the chairperson or secretary of the Appeals Tribunal.
Meetings of Appeals Tribunal.

3\.
(1) The Appeals Tribunal shall meet on such occasions as may be expedient
for the hearing of appeals and at such places, times and on such days as the Appeals
Tribunal may determine.
(2) The chairperson shall preside at all meetings of the Appeals Tribunal.
(3) A quorum of the Appeals Tribunal shall consist of a majority of members,
that is to say, three members, which shall include the chairperson.
(4) The decisions of the Appeals Tribunal shall be by a majority of votes of
members present and voting, and in the event of an equality of votes, the chairperson
shall have a casting vote.
Declaration of interest.

4\.
(1) It shall be the duty of a member of the Appeals Tribunal who is in any way
directly or indirectly interested in a matter coming before the Appeals Tribunal to
declare the nature of his or her interest in the matter as soon as it is practicable for
him or her to do so, and he or she shall remove himself from any meeting of the
Tribunal on that matter, and take no part directly or indirectly in any decision,
deliberation, discussion, consideration or similar activity of the Appeals Tribunal on
that matter.
(2) Where the Appeals Tribunal lacks a quorum in relation to an appeal owing
to the number of members, who have declared an interest in that appeal, the
Governor-General shall, for the purpose of that appeal, revoke the appointment of
those members and appoint other persons in their stead.
Remuneration.
5\.
For each sitting of the Appeals Tribunal the members, other than ex-officio
members, shall be paid such remuneration as may be prescribed by the GovernorGeneral, and such remuneration shall be a charge on the consolidated Fund.

Validity of proceedings.
6\.
The validity of any proceedings of the Appeals Tribunal shall not be affected
by any vacancy among the members thereof or by any defect in the appointment of a
member thereof.
Procedure of the Appeals Tribunal.
7\.
Subject to the provisions of this Schedule, the procedures of the Appeals
Tribunal shall be such as may be determined by the Tribunal.
———————

## SEVENTH SCHEDULE

(Section 55(2))
BUILDING CODE
Short Title.
1\.
These Regulations may be cited as the Building Regulations.
Interpretation.
2\.
In these Regulations, unless the context otherwise requires—

“Act” means the Development Control and Planning Act, Cap. 20.07;
“Board” means the Board established under Section 6 of the Act;
“building” means a structure roofed in and capable of affording protection or shelter;
“building lot” means the land exclusively belonging or intending to belong to any
building in respect of which an application is made to the Board at any time,
whether such land at the time of the application has buildings on it or not;
“dwelling house” means any building used or constructed or adapted to be used for
human habitation and, in case of a building used partly as a dwelling house
and partly for other purposes, that part used as a dwelling house;
“living room” means any room which is ordinarily used for living or sleeping
purposes;
“new building” means any building or part of a building or addition to a building
erected wholly or partially or converted into a dwelling house;
“public place” means any place to which the public have access;
“storey” means that portion of a building contained between any two floors, and if
there be no upper floor, that portion of a building contained between the floor
and the top of the wall or roof-plate, except that the space between the lowest
floor and the ground if not exceeding 6 feet and 6 inches in height, or a cellar
beneath a building or a garret in a building of two or more storeys shall not be
deemed a storey within the meaning of these Regulations.
Submission of Applications to the Board.

3\.

(1) A person who intends to undertake any work in relation to the—
(a) erection;

(b) re-erection;
(c) removal; or
(d) external structural alteration;
of a building shall, before doing so, apply in writing in the Form set forth in the First
Schedule to these Regulations, to the Board for the approval of the proposed work.
to it—

(2) The Board shall, before granting approval, require the applicant to submit
(a) a certified copy of title or deed showing his or her ownership of land;
(b) a plan of the site;
(c) a plan of the proposed work; and
(d) an estimate of the cost of the work.

(3) The applicant shall not commence any work without the approval of the
Board in writing.
Documents to be submitted in Duplicate.
4\.
The applicant shall submit in duplicate all the documents referred to in subregulation (2) of regulation 3, and the Board shall retain one copy of each document,
and return the other copy of each document to the applicant.
Consideration of Applications.
5\.
The Board shall, within fourteen days after the receipt of an application and
subject to the provisions of the Development Control and Planning Act, Cap. 20.07
and these Regulations—

(a) approve or disapprove the application; or
(b) require any alteration to be made in the plan of any building intended
to be—
(i) erected,
(ii) re-constructed, or
(iii) altered,
as to its height or size or as may be necessary for the purpose of securing uniformity
in the appearance of the street on which such building is intended to be erected.
Communication of Decision to the Applicant.
6\.
The Board shall communicate to the applicant its decision by endorsing the
same upon the copy of the application returned to the applicant and, in the case of
disapproval, shall indicate its reasons for the disapproval.
Period in which work must be started.

7\.
(1) The applicant shall commence work approved by the Board within six
months of the date of the approval.
(2) The approval of the Board of any proposed work shall lapse if the work is
not carried out within the six months period.

Building Lots.

8\.
(1) A building lot in any prescribed urban area shall not measure less than
1750 square feet, and in the case of a building lot in any prescribed rural area shall
not measure less than 2500 square feet.
(2) A person shall not erect more than one dwelling house on a building lot
referred to in this regulation.
(3) The street frontage of any building lot shall be at least 39 feet.
(4) A dwelling house and outhouse on a building lot shall not cover more than
two thirds of the surface of the building lot.
(5) No building work shall be done on any building already existing on such a
building lot other than such repairs as do not involve replacement of more than 25%
of the structure.
Building Line.
9\.
The building line of a building abutting on a public place shall, unless the
Board requires a greater distance, be set back 16 feet from the—

(a) center of the road, street or other public place;
(b) center of any projected—
(i) road,
(ii) street, or
(iii) other public place.
Frontage.
10\. A building shall not have a frontage which is less than 18 feet to any public
place.
Air Space in front of Buildings.

11\. (1) Each building shall have an open space of at least 24 feet extending
throughout the whole frontage of the building.
(2) The open space referred to in sub-regulation (1) of this regulation shall be
measured from the front of the building to the boundary of any land or premises
immediately opposite or to the opposite side of any public place on which the
building immediately abuts.
(3) The Board may, in its discretion, reduce the above requirements in respect
of buildings containing premises on the ground floor which are exclusively of a
commercial nature.
Air Space at back of Buildings.

12\. (1) Each building shall have an open space of at least 16 feet to its site
boundary at the rear extending the whole width of the building.
(2) The open space at the rear of a dwelling house referred to in this regulation
shall, in addition, be at least two thirds of the height of the building from the ground
to the eaves.
(3) Notwithstanding sub-regulations (1) and (2) of this regulation,
outbuildings approved by the Board may be erected at the rear of a dwelling house.

Distance of Buildings from side Boundaries.

13\. (1) A person shall not erect a building that has eaves, projecting part or
external wall, as the case may be, within 6 feet from its site boundary at either side of
any building, wall, or fence used or constructed to be used for the separation of
adjoining building or adjoining lands that belong to different owners.
(2) For the purposes of this regulation, connected buildings or group of
buildings shall be regarded as one building.
Projection.
14\. A person shall not erect a building such that any portion of or attachment to
the building permanently projects on or over any public place in such a manner that
in the opinion of the Board it would cause an obstruction or danger to vehicles or
pedestrians.
Encroachment.
15\. No person shall erect any new building without first removing any
encroachment or obstruction which may be in or on the public place in front of the
site where the building is to be erected.
Height of Building.
16\. A person who erects a building intended for human habitation—

(a) if the building is of one storey, shall ensure that the storey is not less
than 9 feet in height from the floor to the ceiling or underside of the
roof;
(b) if the building is of two storeys or more, shall ensure that the lowest
storey is at least 9 feet, and the average height of each of the upper
storeys is at least 8 feet from the floor to the ceiling or the underside of
the roof.
Height of Dwelling Houses above ground.

17\. (1) A person who erects a building that is intended to be used as a dwelling
house shall ensure that—
(a) the lowest floor is raised from the ground at least 18 inches; and
(b) the floor is supported on a masonry with proper and sufficient
ventilation beneath the floor.
(2) Notwithstanding sub-regulation (1) of this regulation, where the frontage
of the building abuts on any public place the building shall be on a continuous dwarf
wall or concrete pillars.
Drainage.
18\. The Board may require that—

(a) the site of any new building intended to be used as a dwelling house
has its drains properly laid to a suitable outfall for the efficient
drainage of any rain or surface water from the site;
(b) the sub-soil of the site of the building referred to in paragraph (a) of
this regulation be effectually drained, whenever the possibility of
dampness of the site renders such a precaution necessary.

Living Rooms.
19\. A person shall not erect a building intended to be used as a dwelling house—

(a) with any living room whose floor area is less than 90 square feet;
(b) with any living room which is not provided with windows open to
external air; and
(c) having any living room with an average height of less than 8 feet.
Kitchens.

20\. (1) A person shall not erect a building that is to contain a kitchen or in which
building fire is intended to be used unless—
(a) the floor of the kitchen or room where fire is to be used is tiled, paved,
concreted, or otherwise made fireproof to the satisfaction of the Board;
and
(b) the chimney of the building referred to in this regulation is above the
continuous roofs to such height as the Board may specify when
granting the application for the erection of the building.
Latrine Accommodation.

21\. (1) Any building intended for use as a dwelling, shop, factory, or as a place of
business shall be provided with any of the latrine facilities specified in sub-regulation
(2) of this regulation, as the Board may approve.
(2) The latrine facilities referred to in sub-regulation (1) of this regulation are
the following—
(a) a water-flush toilet erected on the premises which toilet shall be
connected to a septic tank or proper sewerage system, and constructed
in such a manner as shall be specified in the application approved by
the Board;
(b) a dry earth closet of a type as may be approved by the Board;
(c) a pit latrine of a type approved by the Board.
Garages.
22\. Each garage shall be made of concrete, stone, iron or some other noninflammable material as far as practicable, and shall have at least two ventilation
openings, each of which shall not be less than 1½ square feet, placed in such manner
so as to effect cross ventilation.
Water Supply.
23\. The Board may require to be satisfied that an adequate and safe water supply
is available within a reasonable distance of the building.
Demolition of Buildings.

24\. (1) A person shall not demolish any building or part of the building from any
site without the permission of the Board at least 48 hours before demolition of the
building.
(2) It shall be stated in any application for permission to demolish a building
the present site of the building and the site where it is intended to be re-erected.

Dilapidated or Dangerous Premises.

25\. (1) Where any building is in a dilapidated state or in such a condition, as in the
opinion of the Board is likely to endanger life or property, the Board shall—
(a) give notice in writing to the owner of the building; or
(b) in the case of a vacant building, affix a notice to the front door or some
conspicuous part of the building to repair or demolish the building.
(2) If after the expiry of 30 days from the date of the notice referred to in this
regulation the work ordered by the Board to be done is not commenced and diligently
proceeded with, the Board may enter upon the land and do all such matters and things
for the carrying out of orders contained in the notice at the expense of the owner.
(3) Notwithstanding sub-regulation (2) of this regulation, where the owner of
the building is a destitute person and cannot afford the cost of pulling down or
repairing the building as required by the Board, the Board may cause the necessary
work to be performed at its own cost and expense.
Buildings Unfit for Human Habitation.

26\. (1) If on the representation of the Chairperson of the Board of Health for the
District, the Board is of the opinion that any dwelling house is in a state so dangerous
or injurious to health as to be unfit for human habitation such dwelling house shall be
vacated by any persons inhabiting it within 28 days of the service of a notice issued
by the Board.
(2) It shall be indicated in the notice served under sub-regulation (1) of this
regulation the cause of unfitness to vacate that dwelling house.
(3) The building referred to in this regulation shall not be used for human
habitation until the Board is satisfied it has been rendered fit for human habitation
and issues a certificate to that effect.
Building Standards.
27\. A person who intends to erect a building shall conform to the standards
specified in the Building Code and Building Guidelines set out in Schedule 2 and
Schedule 3 to these Regulations, respectively.
